# FlareADM command reference

Generated by `go run ./tools/docgen > docs/commands.md`. Do not edit by hand.

This reference covers 324 commands.

## `flareadm account get`

Show one account

Show one account. ACCOUNT_ID defaults to the resolved account
(--account-id, profile account_id, FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID, or the single
accessible account).

```text
flareadm account get [ACCOUNT_ID] [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `-h, --help` — help for get

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input
- `--no-paginate` — fetch only the first page
- `--output string` — output format: table, json, yaml, text
- `--page-size int` — number of items per API page (default: 100)
- `--profile string` — profile to use (default: FLAREADM_PROFILE or "default")
- `--raw` — bypass normalization; print the closest Cloudflare API response
- `--timeout duration` — per-request attempt timeout (default: 30s)
- `--verbose` — verbose diagnostics on stderr
- `--yes` — skip confirmation prompts
- `--zone string` — zone name or id (fallback: profile default_zone)

## `flareadm analytics summary get`

Summary values over a single interval

Run the query for DATASET.

Examples:
  flareadm analytics summary get httpRequestsOverviewAdaptiveGroups --query @query.json

--query takes the query object (filters, from, groupBy, stats, to) verbatim.
Results are dataset-shaped and are emitted as returned by the API.

```text
flareadm analytics summary get DATASET [flags]
```

## `flareadm api request`

Send an arbitrary Cloudflare API request

## `flareadm auth login`

Log in with OAuth (Authorization Code + PKCE)

Log in with OAuth so no API token has to be pasted into the environment.

The flow is Authorization Code with PKCE (S256) and a loopback callback. The
redirect URI it uses must be registered on your OAuth client:

  http://127.0.0.1:8976/oauth/callback

Provide the client id with --client-id or the profile key oauth_client_id.
Scopes default to the read-only set; --scopes picks an explicit set and
--all-scopes requests the full catalog. List the scope ids with
`flareadm auth scopes` - they are the values to register in the `scopes`
array of the OAuth client.

In CI use an API token instead (FLAREADM_API_TOKEN): login requires a
terminal, and with --no-browser it prints the authorize URL once and waits
for the callback until --timeout expires.

Tokens are stored owner-only under the OAuth credential directory and are
never printed, logged or included in error text.

```text
flareadm auth login [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--all-scopes` — request every scope in the catalog
- `--callback-host string` — host the loopback callback listens on (default `127.0.0.1`)
- `--callback-port int` — port the loopback callback listens on (default `8976`)
- `--client-id string` — OAuth client id (falls back to the profile oauth_client_id)
- `-h, --help` — help for login
- `--no-browser` — print the authorize URL instead of opening a browser
- `--read-only` — request only read scopes (the default)
- `--scopes string` — comma-separated OAuth scope ids to request (list them with `flareadm auth scopes`)
- `--timeout duration` — how long to wait for the OAuth callback (default `5m0s`)

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input
- `--no-paginate` — fetch only the first page
- `--output string` — output format: table, json, yaml, text
- `--page-size int` — number of items per API page (default: 100)
- `--profile string` — profile to use (default: FLAREADM_PROFILE or "default")
- `--raw` — bypass normalization; print the closest Cloudflare API response
- `--verbose` — verbose diagnostics on stderr
- `--yes` — skip confirmation prompts
- `--zone string` — zone name or id (fallback: profile default_zone)

## `flareadm auth logout`

Revoke the stored OAuth credential and delete it

Log out: revoke the stored refresh token at the Cloudflare revocation
endpoint, then delete the local credential.

If revocation fails with a network error the credential is kept so the
logout can be retried (exit 8). If the endpoint rejects the token the
credential is deleted anyway and the rejection is reported on stderr
(exit 3). --local skips the network call entirely.

API tokens are not affected: they are managed by the caller's environment,
not by FlareADM.

```text
flareadm auth logout [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `-h, --help` — help for logout
- `--local` — delete the local credential without revoking the refresh token

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input
- `--no-paginate` — fetch only the first page
- `--output string` — output format: table, json, yaml, text
- `--page-size int` — number of items per API page (default: 100)
- `--profile string` — profile to use (default: FLAREADM_PROFILE or "default")
- `--raw` — bypass normalization; print the closest Cloudflare API response
- `--timeout duration` — per-request attempt timeout (default: 30s)
- `--verbose` — verbose diagnostics on stderr
- `--yes` — skip confirmation prompts
- `--zone string` — zone name or id (fallback: profile default_zone)

## `flareadm auth scopes`

List the OAuth scope ids this CLI uses

List the OAuth scope ids that `flareadm auth login` can request.

These are the values to put in the `scopes` array when creating the OAuth
client in the Cloudflare dashboard (Manage Account > OAuth clients), and the
same dot-delimited strings the login sends in the authorize request:
registering a scope here is what makes it requestable. The protocol scope
`offline_access` is added by the login itself (it is what makes the server
issue a refresh token) and is not listed; the OIDC `openid`/`offline` scopes
are rejected by Cloudflare for this client type and are never requested.

The DEFAULT column marks the set the login requests when no scope flag is
given (`--read-only` is the default for `auth login`); rows without it are
requested only with `--all-scopes`. `--all` lists every scope id Cloudflare
reports, which is also what `auth login --scopes` accepts.

The command is offline: it reads the catalog embedded in the binary and
never calls the API or reads configuration.

```text
flareadm auth scopes [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--all` — list every scope id Cloudflare reports, not just the ones this CLI requests
- `--category string` — list only the scopes in this category (see the CATEGORY column)
- `-h, --help` — help for scopes
- `--read-only` — list only the scopes the default login requests

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input
- `--no-paginate` — fetch only the first page
- `--output string` — output format: table, json, yaml, text
- `--page-size int` — number of items per API page (default: 100)
- `--profile string` — profile to use (default: FLAREADM_PROFILE or "default")
- `--raw` — bypass normalization; print the closest Cloudflare API response
- `--timeout duration` — per-request attempt timeout (default: 30s)
- `--verbose` — verbose diagnostics on stderr
- `--yes` — skip confirmation prompts
- `--zone string` — zone name or id (fallback: profile default_zone)

## `flareadm auth status`

Show which credential wins for the active profile

Show the credential source that commands will use, without calling the API
and without ever printing token material.

Environment credentials win over stored OAuth credentials, so a profile with
both reports the environment variable (docs/oauth.md §8).

```text
flareadm auth status [flags]
```

## `flareadm auth verify`

Verify the resolved credential

Verify the credential resolved for the active profile.

An API token is checked with GET /user/tokens/verify, which reports the
token object's status. Account-owned API tokens cannot be verified there
(that endpoint answers HTTP 401 for them), so a rejected user-scoped check
falls back to GET /accounts/{account_id}/tokens/verify; the output then
names the check that produced the result, so a 401 can never read as an
expired credential. An OAuth credential is checked with GET /user, because
an OAuth access token has no token object to verify (docs/oauth.md §5 Q4).
The credential itself is never printed.

```text
flareadm auth verify [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `-h, --help` — help for verify

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input
- `--no-paginate` — fetch only the first page
- `--output string` — output format: table, json, yaml, text
- `--page-size int` — number of items per API page (default: 100)
- `--profile string` — profile to use (default: FLAREADM_PROFILE or "default")
- `--raw` — bypass normalization; print the closest Cloudflare API response
- `--timeout duration` — per-request attempt timeout (default: 30s)

## `flareadm cache purge`

Purge cached content of a zone

Purge cached content of a zone: everything, or specific files, hosts,
cache tags or URL prefixes. Destructive: prompts for confirmation unless
--yes is given; --dry-run previews the purge.

```text
flareadm cache purge [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--everything` — purge all cached content of the zone
- `--file stringArray` — purge one file URL (repeatable)
- `-h, --help` — help for purge

## `flareadm certificate create`

Upload a custom certificate

Upload a custom certificate to a zone.

Examples:
  flareadm certificate create --zone example.com --certificate @cert.pem --private-key @key.pem
  flareadm certificate create --zone example.com --certificate @cert.pem --private-key @key.pem \
    --bundle-method optimal --deploy production

--private-key accepts only the @file form: inline secret values are rejected
because process arguments are observable. The key is sent only to the
Cloudflare API and is never printed or logged.

```text
flareadm certificate create [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--bundle-method string` — bundle method (ubiquitous, optimal, force)
- `--certificate string` — certificate PEM bundle, inline or @file
- `--deploy string` — deploy environment (staging, production)
- `-h, --help` — help for create
- `--private-key string` — private key PEM as @file only (inline secret values are rejected)
- `--type string` — certificate type (legacy_custom, sni_custom)

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)

## `flareadm d1 database export`

Export a D1 database

Start a D1 export and print the download location.

Examples:
  flareadm d1 database export <id>
  flareadm d1 database export <id> --download dump.sql

The API currently accepts only --output-format polling; the response
carries a signed URL for the dump. --download fetches that URL and writes
the dump to a file created with owner-only permissions.

```text
flareadm d1 database export DATABASE_ID [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--bookmark string` — export at this time-travel bookmark
- `--download string` — download the dump to this local path
- `--dump-options string` — dump options as a JSON object, inline or @file
- `-h, --help` — help for export
- `--output-format string` — export format (polling) (default `polling`)

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)

## `flareadm d1 database import`

Import into a D1 database

Import SQL into a D1 database.

Examples:
  flareadm d1 database import <id> --file @local.sql
  flareadm d1 database import <id> --file @local.sql --wait
  flareadm d1 database import <id> --action init --filename local.sql

--file runs the whole protocol (init, upload, ingest) and reports every
step on stderr. --action exposes single protocol steps. If --wait is given
and the ingest succeeded but polling does not finish, the command exits 9
and reports the final bookmark. File bytes are never written to debug logs
or error output.

```text
flareadm d1 database import DATABASE_ID [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--action string` — single import step (init, ingest, poll)
- `--bookmark string` — current bookmark for the step
- `--etag string` — upload etag (ingest step)
- `--file string` — SQL file to import (@path or path)
- `--filename string` — filename reported to the API (defaults to the --file basename)
- `-h, --help` — help for import
- `--wait` — poll until the import completes

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input
- `--no-paginate` — fetch only the first page

## `flareadm d1 database query`

Run SQL against a D1 database

Run SQL and print the resulting rows.

Examples:
  flareadm d1 database query <id> --sql 'SELECT * FROM users LIMIT 5'
  flareadm d1 database query <id> --sql @schema.sql --params '[1, "a"]'

--params takes a JSON array (positional) or object (named); --batch takes a
JSON array of statement objects. Parameter payloads are never written to
debug logs or error output.

```text
flareadm d1 database query DATABASE_ID [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--batch string` — batch of statements as a JSON array, inline or @file
- `-h, --help` — help for query

## `flareadm dns record create`

Create a DNS record

Create a DNS record in a zone.

Content-based example:
  flareadm dns record create --zone example.com --type A --name api --content 192.0.2.10 --proxied

Structured example:
  flareadm dns record create --zone example.com --type SRV --name _sip._tcp \
    --priority 10 --weight 5 --port 5060 --target sip.example.com

Structured record types (flags are required unless marked optional):
  CAA       --flags --tag --value
  CERT      --algorithm --cert-type --certificate --key-tag
  DNSKEY    --flags --protocol --algorithm --public-key
  DS        --key-tag --algorithm --digest-type --digest
  HTTPS     --priority --target --value
  LOC       --lat-degrees --lat-minutes --lat-seconds --lat-direction --long-degrees --long-minutes --long-seconds --long-direction --altitude (optional: --precision-horz --precision-vert)
  NAPTR     --order --preference --flags --service --regex --replacement
  SMIMEA    --usage --selector --matching-type --certificate
  SRV       --priority --weight --port --target
  SSHFP     --algorithm --fingerprint-type --fingerprint
  SVCB      --priority --target --value
  TLSA      --usage --selector --matching-type --certificate
  URI       --weight --target [record-level]
OPENPGPKEY uses --content like other content-based types.

```text
flareadm dns record create [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--algorithm int64` — structured "algorithm" field for CERT, DNSKEY, DS, SSHFP records
- `--altitude float64` — structured "altitude" field for LOC records
- `--cert-type int64` — structured "cert-type" field for CERT records
- `--certificate string` — structured "certificate" field for CERT, SMIMEA, TLSA records
- `--comment string` — record comment
- `--content string` — record content (e.g. 192.0.2.10); content-based types only
- `--digest string` — structured "digest" field for DS records
- `--digest-type int64` — structured "digest-type" field for DS records
- `--fingerprint string` — structured "fingerprint" field for SSHFP records
- `--fingerprint-type int64` — structured "fingerprint-type" field for SSHFP records
- `--flags string` — structured "flags" field for CAA, DNSKEY, NAPTR records
- `-h, --help` — help for create
- `--key-tag int64` — structured "key-tag" field for CERT, DS records
- `--lat-degrees float64` — structured "lat-degrees" field for LOC records
- `--lat-direction string` — structured "lat-direction" field for LOC records
- `--lat-minutes float64` — structured "lat-minutes" field for LOC records
- `--lat-seconds float64` — structured "lat-seconds" field for LOC records
- `--long-degrees float64` — structured "long-degrees" field for LOC records
- `--long-direction string` — structured "long-direction" field for LOC records
- `--long-minutes float64` — structured "long-minutes" field for LOC records
- `--long-seconds float64` — structured "long-seconds" field for LOC records
- `--matching-type int64` — structured "matching-type" field for SMIMEA, TLSA records
- `--name string` — record name (may be relative to the zone, e.g. api)
- `--order int64` — structured "order" field for NAPTR records
- `--port int64` — structured "port" field for SRV records
- `--precision-horz float64` — structured "precision-horz" field for LOC records
- `--precision-vert float64` — structured "precision-vert" field for LOC records
- `--preference int64` — structured "preference" field for NAPTR records
- `--priority int` — record priority (MX, URI: record-level; SRV, SVCB, HTTPS: data field)
- `--protocol int64` — structured "protocol" field for DNSKEY records
- `--proxied` — proxy through Cloudflare (A/AAAA/CNAME only)
- `--public-key string` — structured "public-key" field for DNSKEY records
- `--regex string` — structured "regex" field for NAPTR records
- `--replacement string` — structured "replacement" field for NAPTR records
- `--selector int64` — structured "selector" field for SMIMEA, TLSA records
- `--service string` — structured "service" field for NAPTR records
- `--tag string` — structured "tag" field for CAA records
- `--target string` — structured "target" field for SRV, URI, SVCB, HTTPS records
- `--ttl int` — time to live in seconds, or 1 for automatic
- `--type string` — record type (A, AAAA, CNAME, MX, TXT, or a structured type: CAA, CERT, DNSKEY, DS, HTTPS, LOC, NAPTR, OPENPGPKEY, SMIMEA, SRV, SSHFP, SVCB, TLSA, URI)
- `--usage int64` — structured "usage" field for SMIMEA, TLSA records
- `--value string` — structured "value" field for CAA, SVCB, HTTPS records
- `--weight int64` — structured "weight" field for SRV, URI records

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input
- `--no-paginate` — fetch only the first page
- `--output string` — output format: table, json, yaml, text
- `--page-size int` — number of items per API page (default: 100)
- `--profile string` — profile to use (default: FLAREADM_PROFILE or "default")
- `--raw` — bypass normalization; print the closest Cloudflare API response
- `--timeout duration` — per-request attempt timeout (default: 30s)
- `--verbose` — verbose diagnostics on stderr
- `--yes` — skip confirmation prompts
- `--zone string` — zone name or id (fallback: profile default_zone)

## `flareadm dns record update`

Update a DNS record

Update a DNS record by id. Provided flags override the existing record;
omitted fields keep their current values (including structured data fields
when the record type is unchanged).

Structured record types (flags are required unless marked optional):
  CAA       --flags --tag --value
  CERT      --algorithm --cert-type --certificate --key-tag
  DNSKEY    --flags --protocol --algorithm --public-key
  DS        --key-tag --algorithm --digest-type --digest
  HTTPS     --priority --target --value
  LOC       --lat-degrees --lat-minutes --lat-seconds --lat-direction --long-degrees --long-minutes --long-seconds --long-direction --altitude (optional: --precision-horz --precision-vert)
  NAPTR     --order --preference --flags --service --regex --replacement
  SMIMEA    --usage --selector --matching-type --certificate
  SRV       --priority --weight --port --target
  SSHFP     --algorithm --fingerprint-type --fingerprint
  SVCB      --priority --target --value
  TLSA      --usage --selector --matching-type --certificate
  URI       --weight --target [record-level]
OPENPGPKEY uses --content like other content-based types.

```text
flareadm dns record update RECORD_ID [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--algorithm int64` — structured "algorithm" field for CERT, DNSKEY, DS, SSHFP records
- `--altitude float64` — structured "altitude" field for LOC records
- `--cert-type int64` — structured "cert-type" field for CERT records
- `--certificate string` — structured "certificate" field for CERT, SMIMEA, TLSA records
- `--comment string` — record comment
- `--content string` — record content (e.g. 192.0.2.10); content-based types only
- `--digest string` — structured "digest" field for DS records
- `--digest-type int64` — structured "digest-type" field for DS records
- `--fingerprint string` — structured "fingerprint" field for SSHFP records
- `--fingerprint-type int64` — structured "fingerprint-type" field for SSHFP records
- `--flags string` — structured "flags" field for CAA, DNSKEY, NAPTR records
- `-h, --help` — help for update
- `--key-tag int64` — structured "key-tag" field for CERT, DS records
- `--lat-degrees float64` — structured "lat-degrees" field for LOC records
- `--lat-direction string` — structured "lat-direction" field for LOC records
- `--lat-minutes float64` — structured "lat-minutes" field for LOC records
- `--lat-seconds float64` — structured "lat-seconds" field for LOC records
- `--long-degrees float64` — structured "long-degrees" field for LOC records
- `--long-direction string` — structured "long-direction" field for LOC records
- `--long-minutes float64` — structured "long-minutes" field for LOC records
- `--long-seconds float64` — structured "long-seconds" field for LOC records
- `--matching-type int64` — structured "matching-type" field for SMIMEA, TLSA records
- `--name string` — record name (may be relative to the zone, e.g. api)
- `--order int64` — structured "order" field for NAPTR records
- `--port int64` — structured "port" field for SRV records
- `--precision-horz float64` — structured "precision-horz" field for LOC records
- `--precision-vert float64` — structured "precision-vert" field for LOC records
- `--preference int64` — structured "preference" field for NAPTR records
- `--priority int` — record priority (MX, URI: record-level; SRV, SVCB, HTTPS: data field)
- `--protocol int64` — structured "protocol" field for DNSKEY records
- `--proxied` — proxy through Cloudflare (A/AAAA/CNAME only)
- `--public-key string` — structured "public-key" field for DNSKEY records
- `--regex string` — structured "regex" field for NAPTR records
- `--replacement string` — structured "replacement" field for NAPTR records
- `--selector int64` — structured "selector" field for SMIMEA, TLSA records
- `--service string` — structured "service" field for NAPTR records
- `--tag string` — structured "tag" field for CAA records
- `--target string` — structured "target" field for SRV, URI, SVCB, HTTPS records
- `--ttl int` — time to live in seconds, or 1 for automatic
- `--type string` — record type (A, AAAA, CNAME, MX, TXT, or a structured type: CAA, CERT, DNSKEY, DS, HTTPS, LOC, NAPTR, OPENPGPKEY, SMIMEA, SRV, SSHFP, SVCB, TLSA, URI)
- `--usage int64` — structured "usage" field for SMIMEA, TLSA records
- `--value string` — structured "value" field for CAA, SVCB, HTTPS records
- `--weight int64` — structured "weight" field for SRV, URI records

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input
- `--no-paginate` — fetch only the first page
- `--output string` — output format: table, json, yaml, text
- `--page-size int` — number of items per API page (default: 100)
- `--profile string` — profile to use (default: FLAREADM_PROFILE or "default")
- `--raw` — bypass normalization; print the closest Cloudflare API response
- `--timeout duration` — per-request attempt timeout (default: 30s)
- `--verbose` — verbose diagnostics on stderr
- `--yes` — skip confirmation prompts
- `--zone string` — zone name or id (fallback: profile default_zone)

## `flareadm healthcheck create`

Create a health check

Create a zone health check.

Example:
  flareadm healthcheck create --zone example.com --name web --address \
    example.com --type HTTPS --http-config '{"path":"/health"}'

--http-config values under header are registered as protected secrets.

```text
flareadm healthcheck create --name NAME --address HOST --type TYPE [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--address string` — hostname or IP to check (required on create)
- `--check-regions string` — comma-separated regions to probe from
- `--consecutive-fails int64` — failed attempts before marking unhealthy
- `--consecutive-successes int64` — successful attempts before marking healthy
- `--description string` — description
- `-h, --help` — help for create
- `--http-config string` — HTTP configuration object as JSON, inline or @file (header values are treated as credentials)
- `--interval int64` — seconds between checks
- `--name string` — health check name (required on create)
- `--retries int64` — retries before marking an origin unhealthy
- `--settings string` — additional fields as a JSON object, inline or @file
- `--suspended` — suspend the health check
- `--tcp-config string` — TCP configuration object as JSON, inline or @file
- `--timeout int64` — seconds before a check attempt times out
- `--type string` — check type (HTTP, HTTPS, TCP) (required on create)

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input
- `--no-paginate` — fetch only the first page
- `--output string` — output format: table, json, yaml, text
- `--page-size int` — number of items per API page (default: 100)
- `--profile string` — profile to use (default: FLAREADM_PROFILE or "default")
- `--raw` — bypass normalization; print the closest Cloudflare API response
- `--verbose` — verbose diagnostics on stderr
- `--yes` — skip confirmation prompts
- `--zone string` — zone name or id (fallback: profile default_zone)

## `flareadm healthcheck update`

Update a health check

Update a health check. Provided fields are merged into the current check and
the result is PUT, so fields this CLI does not model are preserved.

```text
flareadm healthcheck update HEALTHCHECK_ID [flags]
```

- `--address string` — hostname or IP to check (required on create)
- `--check-regions string` — comma-separated regions to probe from

- `--type string` — check type (HTTP, HTTPS, TCP) (required on create)

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)

## `flareadm hyperdrive config create`

Create a Hyperdrive configuration

Create a Hyperdrive configuration.

Example:
  flareadm hyperdrive config create --name app-db --origin @origin.json \
    --caching '{"disabled":false}' --origin-connection-limit 10

--origin is @file-only: it carries database credentials. Its contents are
never printed, logged or echoed in errors.

```text
flareadm hyperdrive config create --name NAME --origin @origin.json [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--caching string` — caching options as a JSON object, inline or @file
- `-h, --help` — help for create
- `--name string` — configuration name
- `--origin string` — origin configuration JSON as @file only (carries credentials)
- `--origin-connection-limit int64` — maximum origin connections

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input

- `--output string` — output format: table, json, yaml, text

## `flareadm kv key get`

Print a KV value

Print the value of a key. The default, text and --raw output write the
value verbatim; --json and --output yaml wrap it in the normalized
envelope. Values are never written to debug logs.

```text
flareadm kv key get KEY --namespace NAMESPACE_ID [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `-h, --help` — help for get

## `flareadm kv key put`

Write a KV value

Write a key value.

Examples:
  flareadm kv key put config --namespace <id> --value @config.json
  echo hello | flareadm kv key put greeting --namespace <id> --value @-

--value accepts inline text or @file (use @- for stdin). The value is sent
only to the Cloudflare API and is never printed or logged.

```text
flareadm kv key put KEY --namespace NAMESPACE_ID --value VALUE [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--expiration int64` — absolute expiration (unix seconds)
- `--expiration-ttl int64` — expiration relative to now (seconds)
- `-h, --help` — help for put

- `--namespace string` — namespace id (required)
- `--value string` — value to write: inline text or @file (@- for stdin)

## `flareadm load-balancer create`

Create a load balancer

Create a load balancer.

Example:
  flareadm load-balancer create --name www --default-pools <pool-a>,<pool-b> \
    --fallback-pool <pool-c> --proxied

```text

```

**Flags:**

- `--country-pools string` — country pools object as JSON, inline or @file
- `--default-pools string` — comma-separated pool ids used by default (required on create)
- `--description string` — description
- `--enabled` — enable the load balancer (use --enabled=false to disable)
- `--fallback-pool string` — pool id used when no other pool is healthy (required on create)
- `-h, --help` — help for create

- `--proxied` — proxy traffic through Cloudflare
- `--region-pools string` — region pools object as JSON, inline or @file
- `--rules string` — rules array as JSON, inline or @file
- `--session-affinity string` — session affinity (none, cookie, ip_cookie)
- `--settings string` — additional fields as a JSON object, inline or @file
- `--steering-policy string` — steering policy (off, geo, random, dynamic, proximity, least_conn, ...)
- `--ttl int64` — DNS TTL in seconds

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)

## `flareadm load-balancer monitor create`

Create a monitor

Create a monitor.

Example:
  flareadm load-balancer monitor create --type https --path /health \
    --expected-codes 200 --interval 60

--header values are registered as protected secrets.

```text
flareadm load-balancer monitor create --type TYPE [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--allow-insecure` — accept insecure TLS certificates
- `--consecutive-down int64` — failed probes before marking unhealthy
- `--consecutive-up int64` — successful probes before marking healthy
- `--description string` — description
- `--expected-body string` — substring expected in the response body
- `--expected-codes string` — expected HTTP status codes (for example 2xx)
- `--follow-redirects` — follow HTTP redirects
- `--header string` — probe request headers as a JSON object, inline or @file (values are treated as credentials)
- `-h, --help` — help for create
- `--interval int64` — seconds between probes
- `--method string` — HTTP method to probe with
- `--path string` — HTTP path to probe
- `--port int64` — port to probe
- `--probe-zone string` — zone to probe from
- `--retries int64` — retries before marking unhealthy
- `--settings string` — additional monitor fields as a JSON object, inline or @file
- `--timeout int64` — seconds before a probe times out
- `--type string` — probe type: http, https, tcp, udp_icmp, icmp_ping, smtp (required on create)

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input
- `--no-paginate` — fetch only the first page
- `--output string` — output format: table, json, yaml, text
- `--page-size int` — number of items per API page (default: 100)
- `--profile string` — profile to use (default: FLAREADM_PROFILE or "default")
- `--raw` — bypass normalization; print the closest Cloudflare API response
- `--verbose` — verbose diagnostics on stderr
- `--yes` — skip confirmation prompts
- `--zone string` — zone name or id (fallback: profile default_zone)

Create an origin pool.

Example:
  flareadm load-balancer pool create --name primary \
    --origins '[{"name":"a","address":"192.0.2.1"}]'


- `--check-regions string` — comma-separated regions to probe from
- `--description string` — description
- `--enabled` — enable the pool (use --enabled=false to disable)
- `-h, --help` — help for create
- `--load-shedding string` — load shedding object as JSON, inline or @file
- `--minimum-origins int64` — minimum number of healthy origins
- `--monitor string` — monitor id used to probe the origins
- `--name string` — pool name (required on create)
- `--notification-email string` — email notified about pool state changes
- `--origin-steering string` — origin steering object as JSON, inline or @file
- `--origins string` — origins array as JSON, inline or @file (required on create)
- `--settings string` — additional pool fields as a JSON object, inline or @file

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it

## `flareadm logpush job create`

Create a Logpush job

Create a Logpush job.

Example:
  flareadm logpush job create --destination-conf @dest.txt \
    --dataset http_requests --name requests --enabled

--destination-conf is @file-only because the URL embeds destination
credentials; previews name the job and dataset but never the destination.

```text
flareadm logpush job create --destination-conf @dest.txt --dataset DATASET [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--dataset string` — dataset to push (required)
- `--destination-conf string` — destination URL as @file only (it embeds destination credentials)
- `--enabled` — enable the job (use --enabled=false to disable)
- `--filter string` — Logpush filter expression
- `--frequency string` — upload frequency (for example high or 5m)
- `-h, --help` — help for create
- `--kind string` — job kind (for example edge)
- `--logpull-options string` — logpull options string
- `--max-upload-bytes int64` — maximum bytes per upload
- `--max-upload-interval-seconds int64` — maximum seconds between uploads
- `--max-upload-records int64` — maximum records per upload
- `--name string` — job name
- `--output-options string` — output options object as JSON, inline or @file
- `--ownership-challenge string` — destination ownership challenge token as @file only
- `--settings string` — additional job fields as a JSON object, inline or @file

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input
- `--no-paginate` — fetch only the first page
- `--output string` — output format: table, json, yaml, text
- `--page-size int` — number of items per API page (default: 100)
- `--profile string` — profile to use (default: FLAREADM_PROFILE or "default")
- `--raw` — bypass normalization; print the closest Cloudflare API response
- `--timeout duration` — per-request attempt timeout (default: 30s)
- `--verbose` — verbose diagnostics on stderr

## `flareadm logs query`

Run a Log Explorer SQL query

Run a Log Explorer SQL query.

Example:
  flareadm logs query --sql 'SELECT count(*) FROM http_requests' --zone example.com

The SQL text is sent as the request body (text/plain); rows are returned as
they come from the API.

```text
flareadm logs query --sql @query.sql [flags]
```

## `flareadm notifications policy create`

Create a notification policy

Create a notification policy.

Example:
  flareadm notifications policy create --name "origin errors" \
    --alert-type http_alert_origin_error --enabled \
    --mechanisms '{"email":[{"id":"<destination-id>"}]}'

```text
flareadm notifications policy create --name NAME --alert-type TYPE --enabled --mechanisms @mechanisms.json [flags]
```

## `flareadm notifications policy update`

Update a notification policy

Update a policy. Provided fields are merged into the current policy and the
result is PUT, so fields this CLI does not model are preserved.

```text
flareadm notifications policy update POLICY_ID [flags]
```

- `--alert-interval string` — minimum time between notifications

## `flareadm pages project create`

Create a Pages project

Create a Pages project.

Example:
  flareadm pages project create --name docs --production-branch main \
    --build-config '{"build_command":"npm run build","destination_dir":"dist"}'

--deployment-configs and --source are @file-only: deployment configurations
carry environment variable values and source configurations carry repository
credentials. Those values are registered as protected secrets and never appear
in diagnostics, error text or previews.

```text
flareadm pages project create --name NAME --production-branch BRANCH [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--build-config string` — build configuration object as JSON, inline or @file
- `--deployment-configs string` — deployment configuration object as @file only (environment variables are credentials)
- `-h, --help` — help for create
- `--name string` — project name
- `--production-branch string` — production branch
- `--settings string` — additional project fields as a JSON object, inline or @file
- `--source string` — source control configuration object as @file only (repo credentials)

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)

## `flareadm pages project update`

Update a Pages project

Update a Pages project. Provided fields are merged into the current project and
the result is PATCHed, so unmodeled build_config and deployment_configs fields
are preserved.

```text
flareadm pages project update PROJECT [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--build-config string` — build configuration object as JSON, inline or @file
- `--deployment-configs string` — deployment configuration object as @file only (environment variables are credentials)
- `-h, --help` — help for update
- `--name string` — project name
- `--production-branch string` — production branch
- `--settings string` — additional project fields as a JSON object, inline or @file
- `--source string` — source control configuration object as @file only (repo credentials)

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input
- `--no-paginate` — fetch only the first page
- `--output string` — output format: table, json, yaml, text
- `--page-size int` — number of items per API page (default: 100)
- `--profile string` — profile to use (default: FLAREADM_PROFILE or "default")
- `--raw` — bypass normalization; print the closest Cloudflare API response
- `--timeout duration` — per-request attempt timeout (default: 30s)
- `--verbose` — verbose diagnostics on stderr
- `--yes` — skip confirmation prompts
- `--zone string` — zone name or id (fallback: profile default_zone)

given; --dry-run previews the deletion without confirming.

```text
flareadm queue delete QUEUE_ID [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `-h, --help` — help for delete

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input
- `--no-paginate` — fetch only the first page
- `--output string` — output format: table, json, yaml, text
- `--page-size int` — number of items per API page (default: 100)
- `--profile string` — profile to use (default: FLAREADM_PROFILE or "default")
- `--raw` — bypass normalization; print the closest Cloudflare API response
- `--timeout duration` — per-request attempt timeout (default: 30s)
- `--verbose` — verbose diagnostics on stderr
- `--yes` — skip confirmation prompts
- `--zone string` — zone name or id (fallback: profile default_zone)

## `flareadm queue message push`

Publish messages

Publish one message or a batch.

Examples:
  flareadm queue message push <queue-id> --body @payload.json --content-type json
  flareadm queue message push <queue-id> --body 'hello' --content-type text
  flareadm queue message push <queue-id> --bulk @messages.json

--body accepts inline text or @file; --content-type json requires the body to
be valid JSON. --bulk takes a JSON array of message objects
({body, content_type, delay_seconds}). Message payloads are never written
to debug logs or error output.

```text
flareadm queue message push QUEUE_ID (--body VALUE | --bulk @file) [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--body string` — message body, inline or @file
- `--bulk string` — batch of message objects as a JSON array, inline or @file
- `--content-type string` — message content type (text, json) (default `text`)
- `--delay-seconds int64` — delay before delivery in seconds
- `-h, --help` — help for push

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input
- `--no-paginate` — fetch only the first page
- `--output string` — output format: table, json, yaml, text
- `--page-size int` — number of items per API page (default: 100)
- `--profile string` — profile to use (default: FLAREADM_PROFILE or "default")
- `--raw` — bypass normalization; print the closest Cloudflare API response
- `--timeout duration` — per-request attempt timeout (default: 30s)
- `--verbose` — verbose diagnostics on stderr

- `--zone string` — zone name or id (fallback: profile default_zone)

## `flareadm ruleset create`

Create a ruleset

Create a ruleset.

Example:
  flareadm ruleset create --zone example.com --phase http_request_firewall_custom \
    --name "my rules" --kind zone --rules @rules.json

the API to validate the ruleset without persisting it.

## `flareadm ssl setting update`

Update zone SSL/TLS settings

Update one or more SSL/TLS zone settings. Every provided flag is patched as
its own setting; omitted settings are left unchanged.

Values:
  --mode                     off | flexible | full | strict
  --min-tls-version          1.0 | 1.1 | 1.2 | 1.3
  --tls-1-3                  on | off
  --always-use-https         on | off
  --automatic-https-rewrites on | off
  --opportunistic-encryption on | off

```text
flareadm ssl setting update [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--always-use-https string` — value for the always_use_https setting
- `--automatic-https-rewrites string` — value for the automatic_https_rewrites setting

## `flareadm ssl universal disable`

disable Universal SSL for a zone

disable Universal SSL for a zone.

Disabling Universal SSL removes the currently active Universal SSL
certificates from the edge; visitors may lose HTTPS access unless a
custom or advanced certificate is active.

```text
flareadm ssl universal disable [flags]
```

## `flareadm ssl universal enable`

enable Universal SSL for a zone

enable Universal SSL for a zone.

Disabling Universal SSL removes the currently active Universal SSL
certificates from the edge; visitors may lose HTTPS access unless a
custom or advanced certificate is active.

```text
flareadm ssl universal enable [flags]
```

## `flareadm vectorize index create`

Create a Vectorize index

Create a Vectorize index.

Examples:
  flareadm vectorize index create --name docs --dimensions 768 --metric cosine
  flareadm vectorize index create --name docs --preset @preset.json

Use either --dimensions with --metric (cosine, euclidean, dot-product) or a
single --preset.

```text
flareadm vectorize index create --name NAME (--dimensions N --metric METRIC | --preset PRESET) [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--description string` — index description
- `--dimensions int64` — vector dimensions
- `-h, --help` — help for create
- `--metric string` — distance metric (cosine, euclidean, dot-product)
- `--name string` — index name (required)
- `--preset string` — index preset name

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)

## `flareadm vectorize vector insert`

Insert vectors

Insert vectors with an NDJSON payload (one vector per line).

  --vectors is @file-only; the payload is sent verbatim with content type
  application/x-ndjson and is never printed, logged or echoed in errors.

```text
flareadm vectorize vector insert INDEX_NAME --vectors @vectors.ndjson [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `-h, --help` — help for insert
- `--unparsable-behavior string` — behavior for unparsable vectors (error, discard)
- `--vectors string` — NDJSON vector payload, @file only

## `flareadm vectorize vector query`

Query similar vectors

Run a similarity query.

  --vector takes a JSON array of numbers, inline or @file; --filter takes a
  metadata filter object, inline or @file. Vector and filter payloads are
  never written to debug logs or error output.

```text
flareadm vectorize vector query INDEX_NAME --vector '[0.1, 0.2]' [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--filter string` — metadata filter object, inline or @file
- `-h, --help` — help for query
- `--return-metadata string` — metadata to return (none, indexed, all)
- `--return-values` — include vector values in matches
- `--top-k int64` — number of nearest matches to return
- `--vector string` — query vector as a JSON array, inline or @file (required)

## `flareadm waf ruleset update`

Update WAF ruleset overrides

Replace the rules array of a WAF ruleset with the provided overrides.
For Cloudflare-managed rulesets this writes the override list (enabled,
action and action_parameters per rule), which is how managed rule behavior
is customized.

Example:
  flareadm waf ruleset update <ruleset-id> --zone example.com --rules @overrides.json

--dry-run asks the API to validate the overrides without persisting them.

```text
flareadm waf ruleset update RULESET_ID [flags]
```

## `flareadm workers script deployment create`

Deploy script versions

Create a deployment. Either describe it with --strategy and repeated --version
flags, or pass a complete deployment object with --deployment @file.

Example:
  flareadm workers script deployment create hello --version <version-id>=100

The deployment takes effect immediately; --dry-run previews the plan without
deploying.

```text
flareadm workers script deployment create SCRIPT --version VERSION_ID=PERCENTAGE [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--annotations string` — deployment annotations as JSON, inline or @file
- `--deployment string` — complete deployment object as JSON, inline or @file (wins over --strategy/--version)
- `--force` — deploy even when normally blocked (for example when a secret changed)
- `-h, --help` — help for create
- `--strategy string` — deployment strategy (for example percentage)
- `--version stringArray` — version share as VERSION_ID=PERCENTAGE (repeatable)

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)

## `flareadm workers script secret create`

Create or replace a script secret

Create a secret, or replace its value when it already exists (the API's PUT is
an upsert; there is no separate update verb).

--text is @file-only: the value is a credential, is registered as a protected
secret and is never printed, logged or included in previews.

```text
flareadm workers script secret create SCRIPT --name NAME --text @secret.txt [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--format string` — key format (for example jwk)
- `-h, --help` — help for create
- `--key-jwk string` — asymmetric key as a JWK object, inline or @file
- `--name string` — secret name (required)
- `--text string` — secret value as @file only
- `--type string` — secret type (default secret_text)
- `--usages string` — comma-separated key usages

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)

## `flareadm workers script settings update`

Update a script's settings

Update script settings. Provided fields are merged into the current settings
and the result is PATCHed, so fields this CLI does not model are preserved.

--bindings is @file-only because bindings carry credentials (secret_text and
plain_text values are registered as protected secrets).

```text
flareadm workers script settings update SCRIPT [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--annotations string` — annotations object as JSON, inline or @file
- `--bindings string` — bindings array as @file only (secret_text and plain_text values are treated as credentials)
- `--cache-options string` — cache options object as JSON, inline or @file
- `--compatibility-date string` — compatibility date (for example 2026-01-01)
- `--compatibility-flags string` — comma-separated compatibility flags
- `-h, --help` — help for update
- `--limits string` — limits object as JSON, inline or @file
- `--logpush` — enable Logpush for the script
- `--observability string` — observability object as JSON, inline or @file
- `--placement string` — placement object as JSON, inline or @file
- `--settings string` — additional settings as a JSON object, inline or @file
- `--tags string` — comma-separated tags
- `--tail-consumers string` — tail consumers array as JSON, inline or @file
- `--usage-model string` — usage model (standard, bundled, unbound)

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input
- `--no-paginate` — fetch only the first page
- `--output string` — output format: table, json, yaml, text
- `--page-size int` — number of items per API page (default: 100)
- `--profile string` — profile to use (default: FLAREADM_PROFILE or "default")
- `--raw` — bypass normalization; print the closest Cloudflare API response
- `--timeout duration` — per-request attempt timeout (default: 30s)
- `--verbose` — verbose diagnostics on stderr
- `--yes` — skip confirmation prompts
- `--zone string` — zone name or id (fallback: profile default_zone)

## `flareadm workers script update`

Upload a Workers script (create or replace)

Upload a script. The upload is a multipart body: the metadata object plus one
part per --file, whose names are what metadata.main_module (or body_part for
service-worker syntax) references. The script is created when it does not
exist yet.

Example:
  flareadm workers script update hello --file main=@hello.js \
    --metadata @metadata.json

--metadata is @file-only because it carries bindings, including secret_text
values; those values are registered as protected secrets and never appear in
diagnostics, error text or --dry-run previews. Module contents are never
printed either; previews only report the file count and size.

```text
flareadm workers script update SCRIPT --metadata @metadata.json --file NAME=@module.js [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--bindings-inherit string` — set to strict to fail on unresolvable inherit bindings
- `--file stringArray` — module to upload as NAME=@PATH (repeatable)
- `-h, --help` — help for update
- `--metadata string` — upload metadata as @file only (bindings, compatibility date, main_module, ...)

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input
- `--no-paginate` — fetch only the first page
- `--output string` — output format: table, json, yaml, text
- `--page-size int` — number of items per API page (default: 100)
- `--profile string` — profile to use (default: FLAREADM_PROFILE or "default")
- `--raw` — bypass normalization; print the closest Cloudflare API response
- `--timeout duration` — per-request attempt timeout (default: 30s)
- `--verbose` — verbose diagnostics on stderr
- `--yes` — skip confirmation prompts
- `--zone string` — zone name or id (fallback: profile default_zone)

## `flareadm zero-trust access app create`

Create an Access application

Create an Access application.

Example:
  flareadm zero-trust access app create --name wiki --type self_hosted \
    --domain wiki.example.com --session-duration 24h --allowed-idps <idp-id>

--policies takes an inline Cloudflare policies array; --settings accepts any
additional application field as a JSON object.

```text
flareadm zero-trust access app create --name NAME --type TYPE [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--allowed-idps string` — comma-separated identity provider ids allowed to authenticate
- `--app-launcher-visible` — show the application in the App Launcher
- `--auto-redirect-to-identity` — redirect to the identity provider instead of showing a login page
- `--custom-deny-message string` — message shown to denied users
- `--custom-deny-url string` — URL to redirect denied users to
- `--domain string` — application domain (required on create unless the type is SaaS, WARP, infrastructure or MCP portal)
- `--enable-binding-cookie` — bind the session cookie to the client
- `-h, --help` — help for create
- `--http-only-cookie-attribute` — mark the session cookie HTTP-only
- `--logo-url string` — application logo URL
- `--name string` — application name (required on create)
- `--options-preflight-bypass` — bypass Access for CORS preflight requests
- `--path-cookie-attribute` — scope the session cookie to the application path
- `--policies string` — inline policy definitions as a JSON array, inline or @file
- `--saas-app string` — SaaS application definition as a JSON object, inline or @file
- `--same-site-cookie-attribute string` — SameSite cookie attribute (none, lax, strict)
- `--self-hosted-domains string` — comma-separated additional self-hosted domains
- `--service-auth-401-redirect` — redirect service-auth failures to the login page
- `--session-duration string` — session duration (for example 24h)
- `--settings string` — additional application fields as a JSON object, inline or @file
- `--skip-interstitial` — skip the consent interstitial
- `--tags string` — comma-separated tags
- `--type string` — application type: self_hosted, saas, ssh, vnc, app_launcher, warp, biso, bookmark, dash_sso, infrastructure, rdp, mcp, mcp_portal, proxy_endpoint

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input
- `--no-paginate` — fetch only the first page
- `--output string` — output format: table, json, yaml, text
- `--page-size int` — number of items per API page (default: 100)
- `--profile string` — profile to use (default: FLAREADM_PROFILE or "default")
- `--raw` — bypass normalization; print the closest Cloudflare API response
- `--timeout duration` — per-request attempt timeout (default: 30s)
- `--verbose` — verbose diagnostics on stderr
- `--yes` — skip confirmation prompts
- `--zone string` — zone name or id (fallback: profile default_zone)

application and the result is PUT, so fields this CLI does not model are
preserved.


## `flareadm zero-trust access policy create`

Create a reusable Access policy

Create a reusable account-level Access policy.

Example:
  flareadm zero-trust access policy create --name "office only" \
    --decision allow --include @include.json

--include/--exclude/--require take Cloudflare rules arrays verbatim, so rule
criteria this CLI does not model are preserved.

```text
flareadm zero-trust access policy create --name NAME --decision DECISION --include @rules.json [flags]
```

- `--isolation-required` — require isolated browsing

- `--purpose-justification-prompt string` — prompt shown when asking for a justification

- `--require string` — require rules as a JSON array, inline or @file

## `flareadm zero-trust access service-token create`

Create a service token

Create a service token. The response carries the client secret; it is
printed once here (stdout only), registered as a protected secret and is not
retrievable afterwards.

Example:
  flareadm zero-trust access service-token create --name ci --duration 8760h

```text
flareadm zero-trust access service-token create --name NAME [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--client-secret-version int64` — client secret version
- `--duration string` — token lifetime (for example 8760h)
- `--enabled` — enable or disable the token (use --enabled=false to disable)
- `-h, --help` — help for create
- `--name string` — service token name
- `--previous-client-secret-expires-at string` — RFC3339 time until which the previous client secret keeps working

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it

## `flareadm zero-trust access service-token rotate`

Rotate a service token client secret

Rotate the client secret. The new secret is printed once here (stdout only),
registered as a protected secret and is not retrievable afterwards. The old
secret keeps working until --previous-secret-expires-at unless the API default
applies.

Rotating is not destructive but it is a credential change: it is never
previewed with the new value, and --dry-run only reports the intent.


Create a device posture rule

Create a device posture rule.

Example:
  flareadm zero-trust device posture create --name "disk encrypted" \
    --type disk_encryption --input '{"requireAll":true}'


## `flareadm zero-trust device settings update`

Update the account device settings

Update device settings. Provided fields are merged into the current settings and
the result is PUT, so fields this CLI does not model are preserved.

--settings accepts a JSON object (inline or @file) for fields without a
dedicated flag; it wins over flags for the same key.

```text
flareadm zero-trust device settings update [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--disable-for-time float64` — minutes the user can disable the client for (0 keeps the API default)
- `--external-emergency-signal-enabled` — enable the external emergency signal
- `--external-emergency-signal-fingerprint string` — public key fingerprint for the emergency signal
- `--external-emergency-signal-interval string` — emergency signal polling interval (for example 5m)
- `--external-emergency-signal-url string` — external emergency signal URL (must be https)
- `--gateway-proxy-enabled` — route traffic through the Gateway proxy
- `--gateway-udp-proxy-enabled` — route UDP traffic through the Gateway proxy
- `-h, --help` — help for update
- `--root-certificate-installation-enabled` — install the Cloudflare root certificate on devices
- `--settings string` — additional device settings as a JSON object, inline or @file
- `--use-zt-virtual-ip` — use the Zero Trust virtual IP range

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)

## `flareadm zero-trust gateway list update`

Update a Gateway list

Update a Gateway list. Provided fields are merged into the current list and the
result is PUT, so fields this CLI does not model are preserved. --items replaces
the whole item set; use `zero-trust gateway list item create|delete` to add or
remove items without resending them all.

```text
flareadm zero-trust gateway list update LIST_ID [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--description string` — list description
- `-h, --help` — help for update
- `--items string` — replacement items as a JSON array of values or objects, inline or @file
- `--name string` — list name

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input
- `--no-paginate` — fetch only the first page
- `--output string` — output format: table, json, yaml, text

## `flareadm zero-trust gateway location create`

Create a Gateway location

Create a Gateway DNS location.

Example:
  flareadm zero-trust gateway location create --name office \
    --networks 192.0.2.0/24 --client-default

```text
flareadm zero-trust gateway location create --name NAME [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--client-default` — use this location as the client default
- `--dns-destination-ips-id string` — DNS destination IPs id
- `--ecs-support` — enable EDNS client subnet support

## `flareadm zero-trust gateway rule create`

Create a Gateway rule

Create a Gateway policy.

Example:
  flareadm zero-trust gateway rule create --name "block malware" \
    --action block --traffic 'any(dns.security_category[*] in {"malware"})'

```text
flareadm zero-trust gateway rule create --name NAME --action ACTION [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--action string` — rule action: on, off, allow, block, scan, noscan, safesearch, ytrestricted, isolate, noisolate, override, l4_override, egress, resolve, quarantine, redirect
- `--description string` — rule description
- `--device-posture string` — device posture rule ids, comma-separated or an expression
- `--enabled` — enable the rule (use --enabled=false to disable)
- `--expiration-duration int64` — default active duration in minutes (requires --expires-at)
- `--expires-at string` — RFC3339 time when the rule stops applying
- `--filters string` — filter definitions as a JSON array, inline or @file
- `-h, --help` — help for create
- `--identity string` — identity expression (for example any(identity.email matches "@example.com"))
- `--name string` — rule name (required on create)
- `--precedence int64` — evaluation precedence
- `--rule-settings string` — rule settings object as JSON, inline or @file
- `--schedule string` — schedule object as JSON, inline or @file
- `--settings string` — additional rule fields as a JSON object, inline or @file
- `--traffic string` — traffic expression (for example any(net 192.0.2.0/24))

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input
- `--no-paginate` — fetch only the first page
- `--output string` — output format: table, json, yaml, text
- `--page-size int` — number of items per API page (default: 100)
- `--profile string` — profile to use (default: FLAREADM_PROFILE or "default")
- `--raw` — bypass normalization; print the closest Cloudflare API response
- `--timeout duration` — per-request attempt timeout (default: 30s)
- `--verbose` — verbose diagnostics on stderr
- `--yes` — skip confirmation prompts
- `--zone string` — zone name or id (fallback: profile default_zone)

## `flareadm zero-trust organization update`

Update the Zero Trust organization

Update account-level Zero Trust organization settings. Provided fields are
merged into the current organization and the result is PUT, so fields this
CLI does not model are preserved.

--settings accepts a JSON object (inline or @file) for fields without a
dedicated flag; it wins over flags for the same key.

```text
flareadm zero-trust organization update [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `--allow-authenticate-via-warp` — allow authentication via WARP
- `--auth-domain string` — login auth domain
- `--auto-redirect-to-identity` — auto-redirect to the identity provider
- `--deny-unmatched-requests` — deny requests that match no Access policy
- `-h, --help` — help for update
- `--is-ui-read-only` — lock the Zero Trust dashboard (use --is-ui-read-only=false to unlock)
- `--mfa-required-for-all-apps` — require MFA for all Access applications
- `--name string` — organization name
- `--session-duration string` — session duration (for example 24h)
- `--settings string` — additional organization fields as a JSON object, inline or @file
- `--ui-read-only-toggle-reason string` — reason recorded when toggling UI read-only

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input
- `--no-paginate` — fetch only the first page
- `--output string` — output format: table, json, yaml, text
- `--page-size int` — number of items per API page (default: 100)
- `--profile string` — profile to use (default: FLAREADM_PROFILE or "default")
- `--raw` — bypass normalization; print the closest Cloudflare API response
- `--timeout duration` — per-request attempt timeout (default: 30s)
- `--verbose` — verbose diagnostics on stderr
- `--yes` — skip confirmation prompts
- `--zone string` — zone name or id (fallback: profile default_zone)

## `flareadm zero-trust tunnel token`

Print the tunnel token

Print the tunnel token (a credential used to run cloudflared).

The token is written to stdout only and is registered as a secret, so it
never appears in --verbose/--debug diagnostics or error messages. Treat the
output as sensitive: it grants the ability to run the tunnel.

```text
flareadm zero-trust tunnel token TUNNEL_ID [flags]
```

- `--yes` — skip confirmation prompts
- `--zone string` — zone name or id (fallback: profile default_zone)

## `flareadm zone get`

Show one zone

Show one zone. Accepts a zone name or id: the positional argument,
--zone, or the profile default_zone.

```text
flareadm zone get [NAME_OR_ID] [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `-h, --help` — help for get

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input
- `--no-paginate` — fetch only the first page
- `--output string` — output format: table, json, yaml, text
- `--page-size int` — number of items per API page (default: 100)
- `--profile string` — profile to use (default: FLAREADM_PROFILE or "default")
- `--raw` — bypass normalization; print the closest Cloudflare API response
- `--timeout duration` — per-request attempt timeout (default: 30s)
- `--verbose` — verbose diagnostics on stderr
- `--yes` — skip confirmation prompts
- `--zone string` — zone name or id (fallback: profile default_zone)

## `flareadm zone list`

List zones

```text
flareadm zone list [flags]
```

**Flags:**

- `-h, --help` — help for list
- `--name string` — only zones with this exact name
- `--status string` — only zones with this status (initializing, pending, active, moved, deleted, deactivated)
- `--type string` — only zones of this type (full, partial)

**Global flags:**

- `--account-id string` — Cloudflare account id (overrides profile and FLAREADM_ACCOUNT_ID)
- `--debug` — debug diagnostics on stderr (credentials redacted)
- `--dry-run` — preview the operation without executing it
- `--endpoint-url string` — Cloudflare API base URL (default: https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/)
- `--json` — output the normalized JSON envelope (alias for --output json)
- `--max-items int` — stop after this many items (0 = no limit)
- `--no-color` — disable colored output
- `--no-input` — never prompt for input
- `--no-paginate` — fetch only the first page
- `--output string` — output format: table, json, yaml, text
- `--page-size int` — number of items per API page (default: 100)
- `--profile string` — profile to use (default: FLAREADM_PROFILE or "default")
- `--raw` — bypass normalization; print the closest Cloudflare API response
- `--timeout duration` — per-request attempt timeout (default: 30s)
- `--verbose` — verbose diagnostics on stderr
- `--yes` — skip confirmation prompts
- `--zone string` — zone name or id (fallback: profile default_zone)
